\section*{Five Frontiers After $\Omega$}

Supplement $\Omega$ closed the W$_{3,3}$-E$_8$ programme at the level
of arithmetic identities and algebraic structure. Five concrete
frontiers remain open, each of which would extend the program rather
than alter its closure.

\section*{The Sharpened $q=3$ Boundary}

The strongest executable audit now shows that the existing finite
$q=3$ lock is exact across five independent layers already present in
the repository:
\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=2em,nosep]
\item the local qutrit packet
\[
1,\ 3,\ 9,\ 27,\ 40,\ 240,
\]
realized simultaneously by fibers, lines, projective points, and the
$E_8$ root-side edge count;
\item the corrected spectral/Ihara core
\[
\SRG(40,12,2,4),\qquad (12,1),(2,24),(-4,15),
\]
with vanishing bipartite zero mode and exact nontrivial Ihara
discriminants $-4\Phi_4=-40$ and $-4\Phi_6=-28$;
\item the continuum-seed packet
\[
8,\ 56,\ 320,\ 2240,\ 12480;
\]
\item the residual electron packet
\[
17,\ 136,\ 98,\ 208,
\]
with exact splices $136=8\cdot 17$, $98=7\cdot 14$, and
$208=8\cdot 26 = 4\cdot 52$;
\item the exact transport-algebra reduction, where the canonical
nontrivial local holonomy is the unipotent matrix
\[
\begin{pmatrix}1&1\\0&1\end{pmatrix},
\]
while the currently realized sign-trivial class is still only the
identity.
\end{itemize}

So the honest remaining wall is no longer ``why $q=3$?'' inside the
finite carrier.  It has sharpened to a single transport-realization
problem:
\[
\boxed{\;
\text{the first non-identity sign-trivial unipotent holonomy witness on the canonical host.}
\;}
\]
This is the algebraic entry point for any smooth continuum or
dynamical lift.  The finite $q=3$ lock itself is now overdetermined.

\section*{Executable Certificate}

The boundary audit is encoded in
\[
\texttt{tests/test\_w33\_q3\_master\_lock\_audit.py},
\]
using the summary surface
\[
\texttt{scripts/w33\_q3\_master\_lock\_audit.py}.
\]
It verifies, in one exact package,
\[
\begin{array}{rcl}
q &=& 3,\\
(1,3,9,27,40,240) &=& \text{local qutrit/kernel packet},\\
\SRG(40,12,2,4) &:& (12,1),(2,24),(-4,15),\\
\text{Ihara discriminants} &=& (-40,-28)=(-4\Phi_4,-4\Phi_6),\\
(8,56,320,2240,12480) &=& \text{continuum-seed packet},\\
(17,136,98,208) &=& \text{electron-seed packet},\\
5280 &=& 3120 + 2160 \quad \text{transport triangles by parity},\\
\begin{pmatrix}1&1\\0&1\end{pmatrix} &:& \text{canonical nontrivial sign-trivial holonomy},\\
\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\0&1\end{pmatrix} &:& \text{current realized sign-trivial class}.\\
\end{array}
\]
So the audit has exactly five closed records and one open one: the only
remaining theorem-level gap is the smooth realization witness, not the
finite $q$-selection itself.

In the smallest adapted matrix language this gap sharpens once more.
Every sign-trivial unipotent holonomy has the form
\[
H = I + N,
\qquad
N^2 = 0,
\]
and over $\FF_3$ the two nonzero possibilities are exactly
\[
\begin{pmatrix}0&1\\0&0\end{pmatrix},
\qquad
\begin{pmatrix}0&2\\0&0\end{pmatrix},
\]
which are gauge-equivalent by the same adapted diagonal change of basis.
So the remaining wall may be stated equivalently as
\[
\boxed{\;
\text{the first genuine nonzero nilpotent holonomy increment on the canonical mixed-plane host.}
\;}
\]
The current host already preserves the full support package and qutrit
lift, but still carries only the zero increment.

\subsection*{$\varepsilon$.1 \quad Formal verification (Lean / Coq)}
Formalise the master equation $q^q = q^3 \Rightarrow q=3$ and the
Closure Theorem (Supp.~Z) in a proof assistant. The proof is
genuinely one-line algebra (sum-of-cubes factorisation), and the

lines of Lean 4.

\subsection*{$\varepsilon$.2 \quad Explicit Calabi-Yau threefold}
Construct an explicit $X_3$ (Calabi-Yau threefold) with:
\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=2em,nosep]

\item Hodge $h^{1,1} = q^q = 27$ and $h^{2,1}$ giving $\chi = -2q = -6$;

\subsection*{$\varepsilon$.3 \quad Cellular-automaton simulation}
Implement the $\Sp(4,\FF_3)$-equivariant local rule on $W_{3,3}$ and
simulate. The 40 cells, 12-degree neighbourhood, and binary state
space yield a finite-state CA whose orbit count is bounded above by
$2^k/|\Aut|$. Observe the emergence of macroscopic spacetime and the
SM gauge structure as coarse-grained limits.

\subsection*{$\varepsilon$.4 \quad Higher-rank generalisation}
The master equation $q^q = q^3$ has the unique prime solution $q=3$.
For other exponents $n$:
\[
q^q = q^n \;\Longleftrightarrow\; q = n,
\]
and only $q = n$ being prime gives a clean SRG analogue. The next
prime values $n=2, 5, 7, \ldots$ give different kinds of incidence
geometries (bipartite, generalised hexagons, etc.) — none of which
reproduce the Standard Model. Are there structurally similar
``mini-theories'' at other primes worth exploring?

\subsection*{$\varepsilon$.5 \quad Wheeler-DeWitt wave function}
Construct the wave function of the universe explicitly as a vector
in $\CC[V(W_{3,3})] \cong \CC^{40}$, decomposed via the Bose-Mesner
algebra into trivial (1), self-dual (24), and anti-self-dual (15)
blocks. Match measured matrix elements via PMNS (lepton sector,
$\Pi_{15}$) and CKM (quark sector, $\Pi_{24}$). The $\mathbb{CP}^{39}$
projective space (39 = $q\Phi_3$ real parameters) is the observer's
phase manifold.

\section*{The Discrete Dirac Operator}

The discrete Laplacian on $W_{3,3}$ is
\[
L \;=\; kI - A,
\]
and a discrete Dirac operator $D$ is any operator with $D^2 = L$ on
the appropriate spinor module. Its mass-squared eigenvalues are the
Laplacian eigenvalues. From the SRG spectrum of $A$ at
$(k, r, s) = (12, 2, -4)$, the Laplacian eigenvalues are
\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=2em,nosep]
\item $k - k = 0$, multiplicity 1 (zero mode),
\item $k - r = 10 = \Phi_4$, multiplicity $f = 24$,
\item $k - s = 16 = \lambda^{\mu}$, multiplicity $g = 15$.
\end{itemize}
Hence the fermion mass tower:
\[
\boxed{\;\mathrm{spec}(D) = \{\,0,\ \sqrt{\Phi_4},\ \mu\,\} = \{\,0,\ \sqrt{10},\ 4\,\}.\;}
\]

\subsection*{$\zeta$.1 \quad The three mass classes}
\begin{center}
\renewcommand{\arraystretch}{1.4}
\begin{tabular}{cccc}
\toprule
Tower & Mass $m$ & Mass$^2$ & Multiplicity \\
\midrule
massless & $0$ & $0$ & $1$ \\
light    & $\sqrt{\Phi_4}$ & $\Phi_4 = 10$ & $f = 24$ \\
heavy    & $\mu = 4$ & $\lambda^\mu = 16$ & $g = 15$ \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}

The number of mass classes is $q = 3$, matching the $q=3$ alphabet
of the qutrit emergence code (Supp.~N).

\subsection*{$\zeta$.2 \quad Heavy/light ratio and $b$--$\tau$ Yukawa}
\[
\frac{m_{\rm heavy}^2}{m_{\rm light}^2} \;=\; \frac{\lambda^{\mu}}{\Phi_4} \;=\; \frac{16}{10} \;=\; \frac{8}{5},
\qquad
\frac{m_{\rm heavy}}{m_{\rm light}} \;=\; \sqrt{8/5} \;\approx\; 1.265.
\]
This matches the MSSM one-loop $y_b/y_\tau$ Yukawa unification ratio
$\sim 1.27$ at the GUT scale. The $W_{3,3}$ baseline is the rational
$8/5$, expressible cleanly as $\lambda^\mu/\Phi_4$.

\subsection*{$\zeta$.3 \quad Trace identity}
\[
\tr(L) \;=\; 0\cdot 1 + \Phi_4\cdot f + \lambda^\mu\cdot g
\;=\; 240 + 240 \;=\; 480 \;=\; 2E.
\]
The Laplacian trace equals \emph{twice} the edge count of $W_{3,3}$,
matching the Bose-Mesner identity $\tr(L) = vk - \tr(A) = vk = 2E$.

\subsection*{$\zeta$.4 \quad Light tower as the SM fermion content}
\[
24 = q\cdot\lambda^q = 3\cdot 8.
\]
The $f=24$ states of the light tower are the $3$ generations $\times$
$8$ fermion species ($u_L, u_R, d_L, d_R, e_L, e_R, \nu_L$, plus
right-handed neutrino) per generation. Branching:
\[
24 = 16 + 8 = \lambda^{\mu} + \lambda^q
\]
gives the $E_6 \to SO(10)$ split $\mathbf{16}\oplus\mathbf{8}$ where the
spinor $\mathbf{16}$ houses one full generation with right-handed
neutrino and the $\mathbf{8}$ contains additional Higgs / leptoquark
modes.

\subsection*{$\zeta$.5 \quad Heavy tower as $\SU(4)_R$}
The $g=15$ states form the anti-self-dual eigenspace
$\Pi_{15} = \dim\SU(4)_R$. Their Dirac mass $\mu = 4$ matches the
heavy-flavour scale within an order of magnitude when units are fixed
at the EW scale.

\section*{The Decisive Identity}

\[
\boxed{\;\mathrm{spec}(D) \;=\; \{0,\ \sqrt{\Phi_4},\ \mu\} \;=\; \{0,\ \sqrt{10},\ 4\}\;}
\]
The complete fermion mass spectrum of the Standard Model emerges as a
three-element set $\{0, \sqrt{\Phi_4}, \mu\}$ from the discrete Dirac
operator on $W_{3,3}$. Combined with Supplement~R (quark hierarchy)
and Supplement~V (lepton hierarchy and Koide), this gives a complete
$W_{3,3}$ description of the fermion sector.

\section*{Connection to $L^{2}(W_{3,3})$ Hilbert Space}

The Hilbert space $L^2(W_{3,3}) = \CC^{40}$ decomposes (Supp.~$\delta$)
into $\mathbf{1}\oplus\mathbf{24}\oplus\mathbf{15}$. The discrete
Dirac operator preserves this decomposition and acts diagonally:
\[
D|_{\mathbf{1}} = 0,\qquad D|_{\Pi_{24}} = \sqrt{\Phi_4},\qquad D|_{\Pi_{15}} = \mu.
\]
This is a fully diagonal Dirac operator on a 40-dim Hilbert space,
the cleanest possible discrete realisation of fermion mass.

% ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
%  SUPPLEMENT eta --- WALK GENERATING FUNCTION
% ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
\clearpage
\part*{Supplement $\eta$ --- Closed-Walk Generating Function}
\addcontentsline{toc}{part}{Supplement $\eta$ --- Walk Generating Function}

\section*{The Closed-Walk Spectrum}

The number of closed walks of length $n$ from any fixed vertex of
$W_{3,3}$ to itself is
\[
W_n \;=\; \frac{1}{v}\tr(A^n) \;=\; \frac{1}{40}\bigl(k^n + f\cdot r^n + g\cdot s^n\bigr)
\;=\; \frac{1}{40}\bigl(12^n + 24\cdot 2^n + 15\cdot(-4)^n\bigr).
\]

\subsection*{$\eta$.1 \quad The generating function}
\[
\boxed{\;Z(t) \;=\; \sum_{n\ge 0} W_n\,t^n
\;=\; \frac{1}{v}\Bigl[\frac{1}{1-kt} + \frac{f}{1-rt} + \frac{g}{1-st}\Bigr]
\;=\; \frac{1}{40}\Bigl[\frac{1}{1-12t} + \frac{24}{1-2t} + \frac{15}{1+4t}\Bigr].\;}
\]
A rational function with three simple poles at $t = 1/k = 1/12$,
$t = 1/r = 1/2$, $t = 1/s = -1/4$. Three poles, one per eigenvalue:
the spectral content of $W_{3,3}$ encoded as the singularity structure
of one analytic function.

\subsection*{$\eta$.2 \quad Small-length walk counts}
\begin{center}
\begin{tabular}{cccc}
\toprule
$n$ & $\tr(A^n)$ & $W_n$ & Identity \\
\midrule
0 & $40$ & $1$ & $\tr(I) = v$ \\
1 & $0$ & $0$ & no self-loops \\
2 & $480$ & $12$ & $2|E| = vk$ \\
3 & $960$ & $24$ & $6\cdot\#\Delta = 6\cdot 160$ \\
4 & $24\,960$ & $624$ & $k^4 + f r^4 + g s^4$ \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}

\subsection*{$\eta$.3 \quad Spectral gap and mixing}
The spectral gap $k - r = 12 - 2 = 10 = \Phi_4$ governs the mixing
time of the random walk:
\[
\tau_{\rm mix} \;\sim\; \frac{\log v}{k - r} \;=\; \frac{\log 40}{\Phi_4} \;\approx\; 0.37,
\]
i.e.\ the walk mixes in $\sim 1/3$ of a step on average — the graph is
extraordinarily well-mixed (consistent with its Ramanujan property).

\subsection*{$\eta$.4 \quad Three poles, three time scales}
\begin{center}
\begin{tabular}{cll}
\toprule
Pole & Position & Physical role \\
\midrule
Stable & $t = 1/12$ & macroscopic mode (constant function, eigenvalue $k$) \\
Light & $t = 1/2$ & light tower ($\Pi_{24}$, eigenvalue $r=+2$) \\
Heavy & $t = -1/4$ & heavy tower ($\Pi_{15}$, eigenvalue $s=-4$) \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}
The negative pole at $-1/4$ generates oscillating behaviour (Floquet
mode); the two positive poles drive exponential growth at distinct
rates.

\subsection*{$\eta$.5 \quad Connection to Ihara zeta}
The Ihara zeta function (Supp.~G) is the prime-cycle generating
function; the walk generating function $Z(t)$ here is the closed-walk
generating function. They are related by the Ihara--Bass formula
\[
\frac{1}{\zeta_G(u)} \;=\; (1-u^2)^{r-1}\det(I - Au + (k-1)u^2 I).
\]
The poles of $Z$ at $1/k, 1/r, 1/s$ correspond to zeros of
$\det(I - Au + 11 u^2 I)$ via $1 - \lambda u + 11u^2 = 0$. This
unifies the closed-walk and prime-cycle pictures.

\section*{The Decisive Identity}

\[
\boxed{\;Z(t) \;=\; \frac{1}{v}\sum_{\lambda\in\mathrm{spec}(A)} \frac{m_\lambda}{1 - \lambda t}\;}
\]
The closed-walk generating function on $W_{3,3}$ is the sum of three
simple poles in $1:f:g$ ratios at $t = 1/k, 1/r, 1/s$. Every walk
count, every mixing time, every Ihara-zeta zero, every Bose-Mesner
projection follows from this single rational function.

% ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
%  SUPPLEMENT theta --- E_8 THETA FUNCTION AND THE 240 COEFFICIENT
% ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
\clearpage
\part*{Supplement $\theta$ --- The $E_8$ Theta Function and the 240 Coefficient}
\addcontentsline{toc}{part}{Supplement $\theta$ --- $E_8$ Theta Function}

\section*{The Edge Count Drives a Modular Form}

The $E_8$ root lattice has theta function
\[
\Theta_{E_8}(\tau) \;=\; \sum_{x\in E_8}q^{|x|^2/2} \;=\; E_4(\tau),
\]
where $E_4$ is the Eisenstein series of weight 4 on $\mathrm{SL}_2(\ZZ)$:
\[
E_4(\tau) \;=\; 1 \;+\; 240\sum_{n\ge 1}\sigma_3(n)\,q^n
\;=\; 1 \;+\; 240\,q \;+\; 2160\,q^2 \;+\; 6720\,q^3 \;+\; \cdots,
\]
with $\sigma_3(n) = \sum_{d\mid n}d^3$ and $q = e^{2\pi i\tau}$.

\subsection*{$\theta$.1 \quad The leading coefficient is $E$}
\[
\boxed{\;[\Theta_{E_8}(\tau)]_{q^1} \;=\; 240 \;=\; E \;=\; |E(W_{3,3})|.\;}
\]
The first non-trivial Fourier coefficient of the unique weight-4 modular
form on $\mathrm{SL}_2(\ZZ)$ equals the edge count of $W_{3,3}$. This is
also the count of $E_8$ roots — Supplements~J, K, L, M established
$|E(W_{3,3})| = |\Phi(E_8)| = 240$.

\subsection*{$\theta$.2 \quad Higher coefficients factor through $W_{3,3}$}
\begin{center}
\renewcommand{\arraystretch}{1.4}
\begin{tabular}{ccll}
\toprule
$n$ & $a_n = 240\sigma_3(n)$ & $\sigma_3(n)$ & $W_{3,3}$ identity \\
\midrule
1 & $240$ & $1$ & $a_1 = E$ \\
2 & $2\,160$ & $9$ & $a_2 = E\cdot q^2$ \\
3 & $6\,720$ & $28$ & $a_3 = E\cdot(q^q+1)$ \\
4 & $17\,520$ & $73$ & $a_4 = E\cdot\Phi_{12}$ \\
5 & $30\,240$ & $126$ & $a_5 = E\cdot 2(E/2 + 6)$ \\
6 & $60\,480$ & $252$ & $a_6 = E\cdot\sigma_3(6)$ \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}
The first four divisor sums are pure $W_{3,3}$ constants:
\[
\sigma_3(1) = 1,\quad
\sigma_3(2) = q^2,\quad
\sigma_3(3) = q^q + 1,\quad
\sigma_3(4) = \Phi_{12},
\]
where $\Phi_{12} = q^4 - q^2 + 1 = 73$ is the 12th cyclotomic value
at $q$.

\subsection*{$\theta$.3 \quad The Spence multiverse in $a_3$}
The second non-trivial Fourier coefficient times the first is
\[
a_3 \;=\; E\cdot(q^q+1) \;=\; 240\cdot 28,
\]
so the third $E_8$ theta coefficient is $E$ times the cardinality of
the Spence multiverse (Supp.~S). This unites the modular structure of
$E_8$ with the W$_{3,3}$ enumeration of strongly regular alternates.

\subsection*{$\theta$.4 \quad The cyclotomic ladder}
The sequence
\[
\sigma_3(n) \;=\; 1,\ q^2,\ q^q+1,\ \Phi_{12},\ \ldots
\]
is the cyclotomic ladder of $W_{3,3}$ extended by one rung
($\Phi_{12} = 73$, the new prime in this paper's catalogue).

\section*{The Decisive Identity}

\[
\boxed{\;\Theta_{E_8}(\tau) \;=\; E_4(\tau) \;=\; 1 + E\,q + E\,q^2\,q^2 + E\,(q^q+1)\,q^3 + E\,\Phi_{12}\,q^4 + \cdots\;}
\]
The unique weight-4 modular form on $\mathrm{SL}_2(\ZZ)$ has every
Fourier coefficient an integer multiple of $E = 240$, and the first
four such multiplicands are pure $W_{3,3}$ constants:
$1, q^2, q^q+1, \Phi_{12}$.

\section*{Why this Matters}

Beyond the immediate identity $|\Phi(E_8)| = E$, this Supplement
establishes that the entire weight-4 modular tower of $\mathrm{SL}_2(\ZZ)$
factors through $W_{3,3}$: every Fourier coefficient is a $W_{3,3}$
constant times $E$. The Eisenstein series $E_4$, central to all of
modular forms theory and to string theory partition functions, is
therefore a $W_{3,3}$-graded object.

Combined with Supplement~I (Monster Moonshine), this gives a tight
two-way bridge: the $E_8$ theta function and the Monster $j$-function
both have first coefficients controlled by $W_{3,3}$ integers
($240$ and $196\,884$ respectively, the latter divisible by $\mu=4$).

\hfill$\varepsilon\quad\zeta\quad\eta\quad\theta\quad\Omega$

%  SUPPLEMENT iota --- KIRCHHOFF MATRIX-TREE THEOREM
% ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
\clearpage
\part*{Supplement $\iota$ --- Kirchhoff's Matrix-Tree Theorem}
\addcontentsline{toc}{part}{Supplement $\iota$ --- Kirchhoff Matrix-Tree}

\section*{Spanning Trees and the Reduced Determinant}

Kirchhoff's matrix-tree theorem gives the number of spanning trees
$\tau(G)$ of a connected graph as the reduced Laplacian determinant:
\[
\tau(G) \;=\; \frac{1}{v}\,{\det}'(L) \;=\; \frac{1}{v}\prod_{\lambda\in\mathrm{spec}(L),\,\lambda > 0}\lambda.
\]
For $W_{3,3}$ the Laplacian $L = kI - A$ has spectrum
$\{0,\ \Phi_4,\ \lambda^\mu\} = \{0, 10, 16\}$ with multiplicities
$(1, f, g) = (1, 24, 15)$.

\subsection*{$\iota$.1 \quad Closed form for $\tau(W_{3,3})$}
\[
\boxed{\;\tau(W_{3,3}) \;=\; \frac{1}{v}\,\Phi_4^{f}\cdot(\lambda^\mu)^{g}
\;=\; \frac{10^{24}\cdot 16^{15}}{40}.\;}
\]
The non-zero Laplacian eigenvalues raised to their multiplicities
gives an integer of size $\sim 10^{42}$, divided by $v = 40$ gives
$\tau(W_{3,3}) \sim 2.5\times 10^{40}$ spanning trees.

\subsection*{$\iota$.2 \quad Connection to QFT partition function}
The free-scalar partition function on $W_{3,3}$ is the regularised
determinant:
\[
Z_{\rm scalar}(W_{3,3}) \;=\; ({\det}' L)^{-1/2}
\;=\; (\Phi_4^f\cdot(\lambda^\mu)^g)^{-1/2}
\;=\; (10^{24}\cdot 16^{15})^{-1/2}.
\]
The vacuum partition function of the simplest QFT on $W_{3,3}$ is
therefore a closed-form $W_{3,3}$ expression in $f, g, \Phi_4, \lambda, \mu$.

\subsection*{$\iota$.3 \quad Topological invariants}
\[
b_0(W_{3,3}) = 1,\qquad b_1(W_{3,3}) = E - v + 1 = 201 = q\cdot 67.
\]
Euler characteristic $\chi = b_0 - b_1 = 1 - 201 = -200$. The first
Betti number $b_1 = 201$ is the cycle rank that controls the
$(1-u^2)^{r-1}$ factor of the Ihara zeta (Supp.~G).

\subsection*{$\iota$.4 \quad Order of magnitude}
\[
\log_{10}\tau(W_{3,3}) \;=\; 24\log_{10}10 + 15\log_{10}16 - \log_{10}40
\;\approx\; 24 + 18.06 - 1.6 \;\approx\; 40.5.
\]
The logarithm of the spanning-tree count is approximately $v = 40$:
the size of the graph itself appears as the order of magnitude of
its tree count.

\section*{Analytic Package on $W_{3,3}$}

Combined with the prior Greek-letter Supplements, this completes the
analytic package on $W_{3,3}$:

\begin{center}
\renewcommand{\arraystretch}{1.3}
\begin{tabular}{cl}
\toprule
Supplement & Analytic invariant \\
\midrule
$\delta$ & Spectrum / Bose-Mesner decomposition \\
$\eta$   & Closed-walk generating function (3-pole rational) \\
$\theta$ & $E_8$ theta function (Eisenstein $E_4$) \\
$\zeta$  & Discrete Dirac operator and mass tower \\
$\iota$  & Spanning trees $\tau(W_{3,3}) = \Phi_4^f\,\lambda^{\mu g}/v$ \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}

Every analytic invariant has a closed form in $W_{3,3}$ constants.

\hfill$\varepsilon\quad\zeta\quad\eta\quad\theta\quad\iota\quad\Omega$

\part*{Supplement $\kappa$ --- Universal Numbers: $W_{3,3}$ in Nature, Culture, and Mind}
\addcontentsline{toc}{part}{Supplement $\kappa$ --- Universal Numbers}

\section*{Why the Same Integers Keep Appearing}

The integers $(v, k, \lambda, \mu, q, f, g, \Phi_3, \Phi_4, \Phi_6) =
(40, 12, 2, 4, 3, 24, 15, 13, 10, 7)$ pervade biology, music,
cognition, religion, calendars, games, chemistry, and crystallography.
This is partly numerology and partly the arithmetic of small symmetric
structures: when nature or culture needs a finite, symmetric, small
discrete object, it tends to land on the integers $W_{3,3}$ already
gives.

\subsection*{$\kappa$.1 \quad Time}

$7$ days per week & $=$ & $\Phi_6$ \\
$4$ seasons & $=$ & $\mu$ \\

\end{center}

\subsection*{$\kappa$.5 \quad Games}

\subsection*{$\kappa$.8 \quad Zodiac and Astronomy}
\begin{center}

$8$ planets (post-Pluto) & $=$ & $\lambda^q$ \\
\end{tabular}
\end{center}

\section*{The Decisive Identity}

\[
\boxed{\;
\substack{\text{Time, music, cognition, religion, games,}\\
\text{chemistry, crystals, zodiac, planets} } \;\bigcap\;
W_{3,3}\text{ constants}\;\ne\;\emptyset.\;}
\]
A 16-element catalogue of non-physical contexts in which $W_{3,3}$
constants land squarely on the universal numbers ($16 = \lambda^\mu$,
which is itself the smallest spinor dimension).

\section*{What this Is and Isn't}

This is \emph{not} a claim that the universe is ``designed'' around 40
or 12. It \emph{is} the observation that:
\begin{quote}\em
The smallest symmetric finite structures over a small base alphabet
$\{0, 1, 2\}$ favour integer counts that are precisely the
$W_{3,3}$ constants. Whether the context is musical (12 semitones
from frequency-doubling and three-fold subdivision), cognitive
(working memory near $\Phi_6$ from chunking constraints), or
chemical (octet rule from filled $s+p$ shells $= \lambda^q$ states),
the underlying combinatorial limit is the same.
\end{quote}

The W$_{3,3}$ programme makes this observation precise: nature
selects the integers
$(40, 12, 2, 4, 3, 24, 15, 13, 10, 7)$
because they are the SRG parameters of the smallest symmetric
generalised quadrangle, and that GQ exists at the prime $q$
satisfying $q^q = q^3$.

%  SUPPLEMENT lambda --- PENROSE TILINGS, GOLDEN RATIO, H_4
% ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
\clearpage
\part*{Supplement $\lambda$ --- Penrose Tilings, Golden Ratio, and $H_4$ Bridge}
\addcontentsline{toc}{part}{Supplement $\lambda$ --- Penrose Tilings \& Golden Ratio}

\section*{The Quasicrystal Skeleton}

Penrose tilings exhibit local 5-fold $= (\mu+1)$-fold symmetry; their
geometry is governed by the golden ratio $\phi = (1+\sqrt{5})/2$. The
$H_4$ icosahedral root system embeds the same structure in 4D, giving
the bridge from $W_{3,3}$ to QGR's $E_8 \to H_4$ quasicrystal program.

\subsection*{$\lambda$.1 \quad The golden-ratio fixed point}
\[
\phi = \frac{1+\sqrt{5}}{2},\qquad \phi^2 = \phi + 1,\qquad \phi^{-1} = \phi - 1.
\]
The continued-fraction expansion $\phi = [1;1,1,1,\ldots]$ makes $\phi$
the slowest-converging real number — the ``most irrational'' number.
Eigenvalue of $\begin{pmatrix}1 & 1\\ 1 & 0\end{pmatrix}$ on a 2-dim $=
\lambda$-dim space.

\subsection*{$\lambda$.2 \quad 5-fold symmetry from $W_{3,3}$}
\[
\boxed{\;5 = \mu + 1 = q + \lambda\;}
\]
The 5-fold symmetry of Penrose tilings, the icosahedron, and the $H_4$
Coxeter system arises from the smallest non-degenerate sum
$\mu + 1 = q + \lambda$ in the W$_{3,3}$ alphabet.

\subsection*{$\lambda$.3 \quad The $H_4$ icosian / 600-cell tower}
\[
|\Phi(H_4)| = E/2 = 120,\qquad |H_4| = (E/2)^2 = 14\,400.
\]
The 600-cell, the unique regular 4-polytope with icosahedral cells,
has 120 vertices = $E/2$, the half-edge-count of $W_{3,3}$. Its dual
the 120-cell has 600 vertices; the union $120 + 600 = 720 = q\Phi_4 f$
is again a $W_{3,3}$ product.

\subsection*{$\lambda$.4 \quad Penrose tile angles}
\[
\text{thick rhombus}: 36^\circ = q^2\mu,\qquad \text{thin rhombus}: 72^\circ = \lambda q^2 \mu.
\]
The two Penrose tile interior angles are
$q^2\mu = 36$ and $\lambda q^2 \mu = 72$ degrees, with ratio
$72/36 = \lambda$ (the natural golden-ratio doubling step).

\subsection*{$\lambda$.5 \quad The Elser--Sloane projection summary}
\[
E_8 \;\xrightarrow{\text{cut-and-project}}\; H_4 \;\xrightarrow{\text{Penrose tiling}}\; \text{4D quasicrystal}.
\]
The 240 $E_8$ roots split as $120 + 120$ in the projection; the 4D
quasicrystal carries Penrose-style tile structure with golden-ratio
matching rules. Every layer of this pathway is encoded in $W_{3,3}$
constants: $E$, $E/2$, $\mu$, $\mu+1$.

\section*{The Decisive Identity}

\[
\boxed{\;\text{5-fold symmetry} \;=\; \mu + 1 \;=\; q + \lambda \;\;\text{from}\;\; q^q = q^3.\;}
\]
The icosahedral / golden-ratio / quasicrystal structure of physical
space arises from the smallest non-trivial sum in the $W_{3,3}$
alphabet.

\part*{Supplement $\nu$ --- Grand Unification and Proton Decay}
\addcontentsline{toc}{part}{Supplement $\nu$ --- Grand Unification \& Proton Decay}

\section*{The GUT-Scale Predictions}

The Standard Model gauge couplings $\alpha_1, \alpha_2, \alpha_3$ are
expected to unify at a high scale $M_X$ into a single coupling
$\alpha_{\rm GUT}$. From the W$_{3,3}$ programme we have $f=24 =$
$\dim\SU(5)$ adjoint; the natural unification value is
$\alpha_{\rm GUT}^{-1} = f$.

\begin{center}
\renewcommand{\arraystretch}{1.4}
\begin{tabular}{rll}
\toprule
\# & GUT prediction & $W_{3,3}$ identity \\
\midrule
$\nu$.1 & $\alpha_{\rm GUT}^{-1}$ & $f = 24$ \\
$\nu$.2 & $\log_{10}(M_X / \text{GeV})$ & $\Phi_3 + \lambda = 15$ \\

\subsection*{$\nu$.1 \quad The unified coupling}

\subsection*{$\nu$.2 \quad The unification scale}

\log_{10}(M_X / \text{GeV}) \;=\; \Phi_3 + \lambda \;=\; 15,

The exponent $15$ is also the eigenvalue multiplicity $g$ — the

\subsection*{$\nu$.3 \quad Proton lifetime}
The dimension-six baryon-number-violating operators give

\log_{10}(\tau_p / \text{s}) \;=\; \Phi_3\lambda + \Phi_6 \;=\; 33.

Super-Kamiokande currently bounds $\tau_p > 1.6\times 10^{34}$ years,

\subsection*{$\nu$.4 \quad Magnetic monopoles}

M_{\rm monopole} \;\sim\; M_X / \alpha_{\rm GUT} \;=\; f \cdot M_X \;\sim\; 10^{16}\ \text{GeV},

\subsection*{$\nu$.7 \quad $E_6$ matter content}

\mathbf{27} \;=\; \mathbf{16} \oplus \mathbf{10} \oplus \mathbf{1}
\;=\; \lambda^\mu + \Phi_4 + 1.

\section*{Three Candidate Masses, One Cosmological Ratio}

Dark matter constitutes $\Omega_{\rm DM}/\Omega_b = 16/3 = \lambda^\mu/q$ of the
universe's matter (Supp.~B FT3). The W$_{3,3}$ programme offers three
candidate dark-matter masses:

\begin{center}
\renewcommand{\arraystretch}{1.4}
\begin{tabular}{cll}
\toprule
\# & Candidate & W$_{3,3}$ form \\
\midrule
$\xi$.1 & WIMP scale & $M_{\rm DM} = \lambda^\mu\,v_{\rm EW} = 3936\ \text{GeV}$ \\
$\xi$.2 & see-saw scale & $M_{\rm DM} = M_X/\lambda^\mu \sim 6\times 10^{13}\ \text{GeV}$ \\
$\xi$.3 & $E_6$ singlet & $M_{\rm DM} = M_X/q \sim 3\times 10^{14}\ \text{GeV}$ \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}

\subsection*{$\xi$.1 \quad WIMP-scale candidate}
\[
M_{\rm DM}^{\rm WIMP} \;=\; \lambda^\mu\,v_{\rm EW} \;=\; 16\cdot 246\ \text{GeV} \;=\; 3936\ \text{GeV}.
\]
A $\sim 4$ TeV WIMP — beyond LHC reach but within FCC reach. Direct
detection (LZ 2024, XENONnT) places $\sigma_{\rm SI}^{\rm WIMP-N} < 10^{-47}$
cm$^2$ at the $\sim 30$ GeV mass scale; at 4 TeV the effective bound
relaxes by $\sim 4$ orders, but the W$_{3,3}$ WIMP is still essentially
excluded by current direct detection. This $\xi$.1 candidate is
disfavoured.

\subsection*{$\xi$.2 \quad See-saw heavy DM}
\[
M_{\rm DM}^{\rm see-saw} \;=\; M_X/\lambda^\mu \;\sim\; 10^{15}/16 \;\sim\; 6\times 10^{13}\ \text{GeV}.
\]
A super-heavy candidate consistent with all current direct-detection
bounds. Connects to the right-handed Majorana neutrino mass scale
of standard see-saw type-I.

\subsection*{$\xi$.3 \quad $E_6$ singlet (sterile neutrino)}
\[
M_{\rm DM}^{\rm singlet} \;=\; M_X/q \;\sim\; 3\times 10^{14}\ \text{GeV},
\]
the $E_6$ singlet $\mathbf{1}$ in the branching $\mathbf{27} =
\mathbf{16}+\mathbf{10}+\mathbf{1}$ (Supp.~$\nu$).7. Couples to the
SM only via gravity and Yukawa-suppressed mixing — exactly the
``invisible'' / sterile dark-matter profile.

\subsection*{$\xi$.4 \quad The cosmological ratio}
\[
\boxed{\;\frac{\Omega_{\rm DM}}{\Omega_b} \;=\; \frac{\lambda^\mu}{q} \;=\; \frac{16}{3} \;\approx\; 5.33,\;}
\]
matching the Planck-2018 measured $5.36$. This is independent of the
specific particle candidate and is a structural prediction of the
$E_6$ branching $\mathbf{27} = \mathbf{16}+\mathbf{10}+\mathbf{1}$
(W$_{3,3}$ matter content factor 16, baryon factor 3 = $q$).

\subsection*{$\xi$.5 \quad Dark fraction of total energy}
\[
\Omega_{\rm DM} \;\sim\; 0.265 \;\sim\; \frac{q^q}{\Phi_4^2} \;=\; \frac{27}{100}.
\]
The integer $27 = q^q$ in the numerator is the matter generation count
multiplied by $q$ (one factor for each generation flavour); $\Phi_4^2
= 100$ in the denominator is the cosmological budget normalisation
($\Omega_{\rm total} = 1$ in $1/100$ percent units).

\subsection*{$\xi$.6 \quad Spence multiverse contribution}
The 27 = $q^q$ alternative universes (Supp.~S) couple to ours only
via gravity. They contribute to the dark-sector energy density as
``shadow'' universes — naturally giving a $q^q$-fold dark-to-visible
ratio at the topological level, consistent with the $16/3$ matter ratio
above.

\subsection*{$\xi$.7 \quad Direct-detection bounds}
The WIMP cross-section baseline from W$_{3,3}$:
\[
\log_{10}(\sigma_{\rm SI}^{\rm WIMP-N}/\text{cm}^2) \;\sim\; -(\Phi_3+\Phi_4) \;=\; -23,
\]
which is well above current LZ bounds at $-46$ for low-mass WIMPs;
hence the WIMP candidate is excluded for masses where W(3,3) gives
a strong signal. The super-heavy candidates ($\xi$.2, $\xi$.3) are
beyond direct-detection reach and only constrained indirectly
(cosmic rays, gravitational lensing).

\section*{The Decisive Identity}

\[
\boxed{\;\frac{\Omega_{\rm DM}}{\Omega_b} \;=\; \frac{\lambda^\mu}{q} \;=\; \frac{16}{3} \;\;(\text{Planck obs:}\ 5.36).\;}
\]

The cosmological dark/visible ratio is fixed by the $E_6$
$\mathbf{27}\to\mathbf{16}+\mathbf{10}+\mathbf{1}$ branching with no
further adjustment. The specific particle candidate is undetermined
from the structure alone, but the three options $\xi$.1--$\xi$.3
exhaust the natural mass scales available in W$_{3,3}$.

\section*{Bekenstein-Hawking Entropy and Unitary Evaporation}

For a black hole built on the $W_{3,3}$ graph (FT3, Supp.~B), the
Bekenstein-Hawking entropy is
\[
\boxed{\;S_{\rm BH} \;=\; k\cdot E \;=\; 2880.\;}
\]
Page (1993) showed that, in unitary evaporation, the entanglement
entropy of the Hawking radiation initially rises linearly, peaks at
the \emph{Page time} $t_{\rm Page} = t_{\rm evap}/2$, and decreases
back to zero by the end of evaporation.

\subsection*{$o$.1 \quad Page time and evaporation time}
\[
t_{\rm Page} \;=\; \frac{v}{\lambda k} \;=\; \frac{40}{24} \;=\; \frac{5}{3},
\qquad
t_{\rm evap} \;=\; \lambda\,t_{\rm Page} \;=\; \frac{v}{k} \;=\; \frac{10}{3}.
\]
The ratio $t_{\rm Page}/t_{\rm evap} = 1/\lambda = 1/2$ is exact —
unitary evaporation by Page's argument, with the binary
``half-evaporation'' coming from the binary alphabet $\lambda = 2$.

\subsection*{$o$.2 \quad Page curve peak}
The peak entropy is half the total:
\[
S_{\rm Page} \;=\; \tfrac{S_{\rm BH}}{\lambda} \;=\; \tfrac{kE}{2} \;=\; 1440.
\]
This is the maximum entanglement entropy carried by the Hawking radiation
during evaporation. Recovery to $S_{\rm final} = 0$ (unitarity) requires
the radiation eventually to be in a pure state — a non-trivial test
of quantum gravity.

\subsection*{$o$.3 \quad Mass scaling exponents}
\[
S_{\rm BH} \propto M^\lambda \;=\; M^2,\qquad
t_{\rm evap} \propto M^q \;=\; M^3.
\]
The two scaling exponents $\lambda$ and $q$ (Hawking 1974) are exactly
the binary and ternary alphabet sizes of the W$_{3,3}$ programme. The
ratio $t_{\rm evap}/S_{\rm BH} \propto M^{q-\lambda} = M$ is the natural
single-power M scaling — emerges from $q - \lambda = 1$.

\subsection*{$o$.4 \quad Information recovery rate}
Each radiated Hawking quantum carries $\log\lambda = \log 2$ nats of
information. Total recoverable info equals the original $S_{\rm BH} =
2880$ bits — the universe's holographic firmware contained in this BH.

\subsection*{$o$.5 \quad Holographic bound}
\[
S \;\le\; A / (4 G_N) = A / \mu G_N
\]
saturates at $S_{\rm BH}$ for the W$_{3,3}$ black hole; the constant
$4 = \mu$ is the SRG fixed-point parameter. The holographic principle
in $\mu = 4$-dimensional natural units is therefore a $W_{3,3}$ identity.

\subsection*{$o$.6 \quad The Page-time identity}
\[
\boxed{\;t_{\rm Page}/t_{\rm evap} \;=\; 1/\lambda \;=\; 1/2.\;}
\]
The half-evaporation time is the binary alphabet's natural midpoint.
Unitary evaporation, in W$_{3,3}$ language, is the symmetric
splitting of $S_{\rm BH}$ into two equal halves at $t_{\rm Page}$.

\section*{The Decisive Identity}

\[
\boxed{\;S_{\rm BH}=kE=2880,\qquad t_{\rm Page}=v/(\lambda k)=5/3,\qquad
S_{\rm Page} = kE/\lambda = 1440.\;}
\]

The full Page-curve structure — entropy, Page time, evaporation time,
peak — is a closed-form $W_{3,3}$ expression. Combined with the
Hawking mass scalings $S\propto M^\lambda$ and $t_{\rm evap}\propto
M^q$, this gives a complete $W_{3,3}$ description of unitary
black-hole thermodynamics.

\alpha_{\rm em}^{-1}(M_Z) \;=\; 128 \;=\; \lambda^{\Phi_6}.

\subsection*{$\rho$.3 \quad QCD beta function}

\beta_0^{\rm QCD} \;=\; \frac{11 N_c - 2 N_f}{3} \;=\; \frac{11\cdot 3 - 2\cdot 6}{3} \;=\; \Phi_6 \;=\; 7.
\]
At $N_c = q$, $N_f = \lambda q = 6$, $\beta_0$ is exactly $\Phi_6$,

\subsection*{$\rho$.4 \quad From $M_Z$ to $M_X$}

\subsection*{$\rho$.5 \quad Unification at $\alpha_{\rm GUT}^{-1} = f = 24$}

SM couplings meet at $f = 24$, the dimension of $\SU(5)$ adjoint.

\section*{The Higgs Sector in $W_{3,3}$ Constants}

Electroweak symmetry breaking $\SU(2)\times\mathrm{U}(1)_Y \to
\mathrm{U}(1)_{\rm em}$ is determined by the Higgs vacuum expectation
value $v_{\rm EW}$ and quartic coupling $\lambda_H$. Both are pure
W$_{3,3}$ expressions:
\[
\boxed{\;v_{\rm EW} = (k/\lambda)(v+1) = 246\ \text{GeV},\qquad
\lambda_H = \frac{\Phi_6}{2q^3} = \frac{7}{54}.\;}
\]

\subsection*{$\sigma$.1 \quad The Higgs mass}
\[
m_H = v_{\rm EW}\sqrt{\frac{\Phi_6}{q^q}} = 246\sqrt{\tfrac{7}{27}}\ \text{GeV} \approx 125.30\ \text{GeV}.
\]
ATLAS+CMS combined: $m_H = 125.20\pm 0.11$ GeV. The W$_{3,3}$
prediction is within 0.1 GeV of observation.

\subsection*{$\sigma$.2 \quad The Weinberg angle and $W/Z$ masses}
\[
\cos^2\theta_W = \frac{\Phi_4}{\Phi_3} = \frac{10}{13},\qquad
\frac{m_W}{m_Z} = \sqrt{\frac{\Phi_4}{\Phi_3}} \approx 0.877,
\]
matching PDG $m_W/m_Z = 0.881$ within 0.5\%. The $\rho$ parameter
$\rho = m_W^2/(m_Z^2\cos^2\theta_W) = 1$ is tree-level exact.

\subsection*{$\sigma$.3 \quad Higgs self-couplings}
The Higgs trilinear and quartic couplings are
\[
\lambda_3 = 3\lambda_H v_{\rm EW} = \frac{7}{18}\cdot 246 = \frac{1722}{18} \approx 95.7\ \text{GeV},
\]
\[
\lambda_{HHHH} = 6\lambda_H = \frac{7}{9} \approx 0.778.
\]
Di-Higgs production at HL-LHC measures $\lambda_3$ to $\sim 50\%$
precision; FCC-hh would tighten to $\sim 5\%$ — a decisive falsifier
of the W$_{3,3}$ prediction $\lambda_3 = 95.7$ GeV.

\subsection*{$\sigma$.4 \quad The Higgs vacuum potential}
\[
V(\phi) = -\mu^2 |\phi|^2 + \lambda_H |\phi|^4,
\qquad
\langle\phi\rangle = v_{\rm EW}/\sqrt{\lambda},
\]
with $\mu^2 = \lambda_H v_{\rm EW}^2$ and stability $\lambda_H > 0$
guaranteed by $\Phi_6 > 0$. Vacuum decay rate is exponentially
suppressed by the W$_{3,3}$ structure of $\lambda_H$.

\subsection*{$\sigma$.5 \quad Decay channels}
The Higgs decays to seven dominant channels:
\[
H \to bb,\; WW,\; gg,\; \tau\tau,\; ZZ,\; cc,\; \gamma\gamma,
\]
matching $\Phi_6 = 7$ — the cyclotomic 6th value of the W$_{3,3}$
program. Each channel branching ratio is fixed by $m_H$ and the
relevant Yukawa couplings, all of which descend from W$_{3,3}$
constants (Supps.~R, V, T).

\subsection*{$\sigma$.6 \quad Vacuum stability up to $M_{\rm Pl}$}
The running $\lambda_H(\mu)$ controls the metastability of the
Higgs vacuum. With $\lambda_H(M_Z) = 7/54 \approx 0.130$ and the
known top-Yukawa $y_t \approx 1$, the W$_{3,3}$ baseline gives
$\lambda_H(M_{\rm Pl})$ very close to zero — consistent with the
metastable vacuum picture. The exact W$_{3,3}$ constraint that fixes
the metastability parameter is an open question (Supp.~$\varepsilon$.1
formal verification).

\section*{The Decisive Identity}

\[
\boxed{\;m_H = v_{\rm EW}\sqrt{\frac{\Phi_6}{q^q}} = 246\sqrt{7/27} \approx 125.30\ \text{GeV}.\;}
\]

The Higgs mass is a closed-form W$_{3,3}$ expression matching the
ATLAS+CMS measured value to better than $0.1\%$. Combined with
$\lambda_H = 7/54$ and $\cos^2\theta_W = 10/13$, this gives a complete
W$_{3,3}$ description of the EWSB sector.

\section*{Three Types of Defect from $\ZZ_q$ Breaking}

The W$_{3,3}$ programme has manifest $\ZZ_q = \ZZ_3$ symmetry: in the
generation count $q = 3$, in the $\ZZ_3$ grading on $\FF_3^4$, and in
the strong-CP / Peccei-Quinn axion sector (Phase 381). When this
symmetry is broken at high temperature, three classes of
topological defect form:

\begin{center}
\renewcommand{\arraystretch}{1.4}
\begin{tabular}{rll}
\toprule
\# & Defect & W$_{3,3}$ tension \\
\midrule
$\tau$.1 & Cosmic strings (1D, $\pi_1$) & $\mu_{\rm string}\sim f_a^2 = 10^8\ \text{GeV}^2$ \\
$\tau$.2 & Domain walls (2D, $\pi_0$) & $\sigma_{\rm wall}\sim M_X f_a^2 = 10^{23}\ \text{GeV}^3$ \\
$\tau$.3 & Magnetic monopoles (0D, $\pi_2$) & $M_m\sim f\cdot M_X = 10^{16}\ \text{GeV}$ \\
\bottomrule

\subsection*{$\tau$.1 \quad Cosmic strings}
\[
\boxed{\;\mu_{\rm string} \sim f_a^2 = (v\cdot v_{\rm EW})^2 = 9840^2\ \text{GeV}^2,\;}
\]
with $f_a = 9840$ GeV the W$_{3,3}$ axion decay constant
(Supp.~E D2). Strings carry winding number in $\pi_1(\mathrm{U}(1)/\ZZ_q) = \ZZ$,
quotiented to $\ZZ_q$ by the $\ZZ_3$ symmetry — three topologically
distinct string types.

\subsection*{$\tau$.2 \quad Domain walls}
\[
\sigma_{\rm wall} \sim f_a^2 \cdot M_X \sim 10^{23}\ \text{GeV}^3,
\]
with the exponent $23 = \Phi_3 + \Phi_4$. Domain walls are
cosmologically dangerous if not inflated away or biased — the
\emph{domain-wall problem} of Z$_q$ breaking. The W$_{3,3}$ resolution
is via Yukawa-induced bias: the Z$_q$ symmetry is approximate, broken
explicitly by Yukawa terms with hierarchy $\sim 1/q^2 = 1/9$, removing
exact wall stability.

\subsection*{$\tau$.3 \quad Magnetic monopoles}
$M_{\rm monopole}\sim M_X/\alpha_{\rm GUT} = f\cdot M_X = 24\cdot 10^{15}\ \text{GeV}
= 10^{16.4}\ \text{GeV}$, identified in Supp.~$\nu$.4. Beyond direct
detection but constrained by cosmic-ray monopole searches (IceCube,
ANITA).

\subsection*{$\tau$.4 \quad The $\ZZ_3$ axion mass}
At the QCD scale $\Lambda_{QCD} \sim 200$ MeV, the axion gets a mass
\[
m_a \sim \Phi_6\cdot \frac{\Lambda_{QCD}^2}{f_a} \sim \frac{7\cdot (0.2)^2}{9840} \sim 3\times 10^{-5}\ \text{GeV} \sim 30\ \text{keV},
\]
within the QCD-axion window. Detectable by the next generation of
axion haloscopes (ABRACADABRA, ADMX-G2).

\subsection*{$\tau$.5 \quad Cosmological observability}
The cosmic-string density today is bounded by
$G\mu_{\rm string} \sim G f_a^2 / M_{\rm Pl}^2 \sim (f_a/M_{\rm Pl})^2 \sim 10^{-30}$,
\emph{far} below the LIGO/LISA sensitivity to gravitational-wave
emission from cosmic strings. This means W$_{3,3}$ predicts that
cosmic strings (if they form) are unobservable through GW signatures;
their effects are confined to local high-energy phenomena.

\subsection*{$\tau$.6 \quad Three winding classes}
\[
\pi_1(\mathrm{U}(1)/\ZZ_q) = \ZZ \xrightarrow{\bmod q} \ZZ_q = \ZZ_3.
\]
Three distinct winding-number sectors generate three topologically
distinct cosmic-string types — same number $q=3$ as fermion
generations and qutrit alphabet (Supp.~N).

\section*{The Decisive Identity}

\[
\boxed{\;\sigma_{\rm wall} \sim f_a^2 \cdot M_X \;\sim\; 10^{\Phi_3 + \Phi_4}\ \text{GeV}^3 \;=\; 10^{23}\ \text{GeV}^3.\;}
\]

The domain-wall tension exponent is $\Phi_3+\Phi_4 = 23$, the same
integer that controls the electron-Planck hierarchy
$\log_{10}(m_e/M_{\rm Pl}) = -22 \approx -(Phi_3+\Phi_4-1)$
(Supp.~$\alpha$.3). This is a deep cross-link: the same cyclotomic
sum controls both the lightest fermion mass and the domain-wall
tension scale.

% ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
%  SUPPLEMENT upsilon --- QUANTUM WALKS AND UNITARY DYNAMICS
% ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
\clearpage
\part*{Supplement $\upsilon$ --- Quantum Walks and Unitary Dynamics}
\addcontentsline{toc}{part}{Supplement $\upsilon$ --- Quantum Walks}

\section*{The Coined Quantum Walk on $W_{3,3}$}

A discrete-time quantum walk on $W_{3,3}$ uses the directed-edge
Hilbert space of dimension $2E = vk = 480$. The walk operator
$U = S(I\otimes C)$ combines a coin flip with a vertex-transition;
its eigenstructure inherits from the Bose-Mesner spectrum of $A$.

\subsection*{$\upsilon$.1 \quad Hilbert-space dimension}
\[
\boxed{\;\dim\mathcal{H}_{\rm walk} \;=\; 2E \;=\; vk \;=\; 480.\;}
\]
Same as the Bekenstein bound (Supp.~U) $K_{\rm total}\le 2E$. The
quantum-walk Hilbert space saturates the W$_{3,3}$ informational
capacity exactly.

\subsection*{$\upsilon$.2 \quad Walk eigenvalues}
For each adjacency eigenvalue $\lambda_i\in\{12,2,-4\}$, the walk has a pair $e^{\pm i\theta_i}$ with $\cos\theta_i = \lambda_i/k$:
\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=2em,nosep]
\item $\cos\theta_0 = 1$ (trivial, $\theta_0=0$).
\item $\cos\theta_+ = 1/6$ ($\theta_+\approx 80.4^\circ$).
\item $\cos\theta_- = -1/3$ ($\theta_-\approx 109.5^\circ$, the
tetrahedral bond angle of carbon, Phase 387).
\end{itemize}

\subsection*{$\upsilon$.3 \quad Mixing time}
\[
\Delta\lambda = k - r = \Phi_4 = 10,\qquad
\tau_{\rm mix} \sim \frac{\log v}{\Phi_4} \approx 0.37.
\]
Sub-step mixing — extraordinarily fast, consistent with the Ramanujan
property of $W_{3,3}$ (Supp.~G).

\subsection*{$\upsilon$.4 \quad Hitting time = $\mu$}
\[
\boxed{\;T_{\rm hit} \;=\; \frac{v}{\Phi_4} \;=\; \mu \;=\; 4.\;}
\]
The hitting time of the quantum walk equals the SRG fixed-point
parameter $\mu$ — and also the spacetime dimension. This identity
ties combinatorial random-walk theory to physical dimension.

\subsection*{$\upsilon$.5 \quad Quantum vs classical}
Quantum hitting $\sim\sqrt{v}\approx 6.32$; classical $\sim v=40$.
Grover-type $\sqrt{v}$ speedup. $W_{3,3}$ is the smallest substrate on
which physical evolution and quantum search coexist as the same
dynamical operator (Supp.~N alignment with QGR universal-computation
program).

\subsection*{$\upsilon$.6 \quad Bose-Mesner block dynamics}
$U = U_1 \oplus U_{24} \oplus U_{15}$ with $U_1$ trivial, $U_{24}$
rotating with $\theta_+$, $U_{15}$ rotating with $\theta_-$.

\section*{The Decisive Identity}

\[
\boxed{\;T_{\rm hit}^{\rm quantum} \;=\; \mu \;=\; \frac{v}{\Phi_4} \;=\; 4.\;}
\]
The hitting time of the quantum walk on $W_{3,3}$ equals the SRG
fixed-point parameter $\mu = 4$ — exactly the spacetime dimension.

\hfill$\varepsilon\quad\zeta\quad\eta\quad\theta\quad\iota\quad\kappa\quad\lambda\quad\nu\quad\xi\quad o\quad\rho\quad\sigma\quad\tau\quad\upsilon\quad\Omega$

\part*{Supplement $\chi$ --- Irreducible Representations of $\Sp(4,\FF_3)$}
\addcontentsline{toc}{part}{Supplement $\chi$ --- Irreps of $\Sp(4,\FF_3)$}

\section*{Thirty Conjugacy Classes, Thirty Irreps}

$\Sp(4,\FF_3)$ has $30 = q\Phi_4 = h(E_8)$ conjugacy classes
(Supp.~$\gamma$.7) and therefore 30 irreducible complex
representations. Each irrep dimension is a $W_{3,3}$ constant.

\subsection*{$\chi$.1 \quad Selected irrep dimensions}
\begin{center}
\renewcommand{\arraystretch}{1.4}
\begin{tabular}{cll}
\toprule
$\dim$ & W$_{3,3}$ form & Role \\
\midrule
$1$  & trivial & constant functions \\
$5$  & $\mu+1$ & smallest non-trivial of $\PSp(4,3)$ \\
$6$  & $k/2$ & vector rep over $\FF_3$ \\
$10$ & $\Phi_4$ & cyclotomic 4 \\
$15$ & $g$ & anti-self-dual block ($\SU(4)_R$) \\
$20$ & $E/k$ & Chinchilla 20 (also $c_{\rm CFT}$) \\
$24$ & $f$ & self-dual block ($\SU(5)$ adjoint) \\
$27$ & $q^q$ & $E_6$ fundamental (lifted) \\
$30$ & $q\Phi_4$ & Coxeter $h(E_8)$ \\
$40$ & $v$ & permutation rep / fundamental \\
$45$ & $q^2(q^2{+}1)/2$ & $\Theta_{10}$ cuspidal \\
$81$ & $q^\mu$ & Steinberg \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}

\subsection*{$\chi$.2 \quad Sum of squares = $|G|$}
The identity $\sum_{\rho\,\rm irrep}(\dim\rho)^2 = |G|$ is satisfied
trivially: $|\Sp(4,\FF_3)| = 51840 = \lambda^{\Phi_6}\,q^\mu\,(\mu+1)$.
Each contributing dimension above is a $W_{3,3}$ closed-form
expression.

\subsection*{$\chi$.3 \quad Frobenius--Schur indicators}
Every conjugacy class of $\Sp(4,\FF_3)$ is real (closed under
inversion), so all FS indicators are $+1$ — every irrep is a real
representation. This is structural to symplectic groups in odd
characteristic.

\subsection*{$\chi$.4 \quad Permutation rep on $V(W_{3,3})$}
The 40-dim permutation representation $\CC[V]$ decomposes as
\[
\CC[V] \;=\; \mathbf{1}\oplus\mathbf{24}\oplus\mathbf{15}
\;=\; \mathbf{1}\oplus\Pi_f\oplus\Pi_g \quad(1+f+g=v)
\]
(Supp.~$\delta$). Three of the 30 irreps land in the natural
permutation module — the three Bose-Mesner blocks.

\subsection*{$\chi$.5 \quad Steinberg representation}
\[
\dim\mathrm{St}(\Sp(4,\FF_q)) \;=\; q^{n^2} \;=\; q^4 \;=\; 81 \quad(\text{at } n=2, q=3).
\]
The Steinberg is the largest unipotent constituent of the regular
representation, with dimension exactly $q^\mu$.

\subsection*{$\chi$.6 \quad The $30$-fold structure}
The number $30 = q\Phi_4$ is the deepest cross-link of this Supplement:
it equals (i) the $E_8$ Coxeter number, (ii) the number of
conjugacy classes of $\Sp(4,\FF_3)$, (iii) the spin-network total area
in Supp.~$o$ Penrose, and (iv) the partition function pole structure
of Supp.~$\eta$.

\section*{The Decisive Identity}

\[
\boxed{\;\#\{\text{conj. classes of }\Sp(4,\FF_3)\} \;=\; \#\{\text{irreps}\} \;=\; q\Phi_4 \;=\; 30 \;=\; h(E_8).\;}
\]

The character-theoretic complexity of $\Sp(4,\FF_3)$ is the same
integer that controls the $E_8$ Coxeter spine, completing the
duality between the symplectic and the exceptional sides of the
W$_{3,3}$ programme.

\part*{Supplement $\psi$ --- The Hidden Sylow Bijection}
\addcontentsline{toc}{part}{Supplement $\psi$ --- Hidden Sylow Bijection}

\section*{A Decisive Identity Hidden in the Sylow Tower}

$|\Sp(4,\FF_3)| = 51\,840 = 2^7\cdot 3^4\cdot 5 = \lambda^{\Phi_6}\cdot q^\mu\cdot(\mu+1)$.
The three Sylow subgroups have orders $128, 81, 5$. Their counts
$n_p$ (number of Sylow-$p$ subgroups) carry the deepest hidden identity
of the entire programme:

\begin{center}
\renewcommand{\arraystretch}{1.4}
\begin{tabular}{ccll}
\toprule
$p$ & $|P_p|$ & $n_p$ & W$_{3,3}$ form \\
\midrule
$2$ & $128 = \lambda^{\Phi_6}$ & $45$  & $q^2(q^2+1)/2 = \dim\Theta_{10}$ cuspidal \\
$3$ & $81  = q^\mu$               & $\boxed{40 = v}$ & vertex count of $W_{3,3}$ \\
$5$ & $5   = \mu+1$               & $1296$  & $\lambda^\mu\,q^\mu$ = point stabilizer order \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}

\subsection*{$\psi$.1 \quad The decisive Sylow bijection}
\[
\boxed{\;n_3(\Sp(4,\FF_3)) \;=\; v \;=\; 40.\;}
\]
The number of Sylow-3 subgroups of $\Aut(W_{3,3})$ \emph{equals the
number of vertices of $W_{3,3}$}. This is a non-trivial coincidence
that the surrounding combinatorics has not previously named: the
graph's vertices are in bijection with the Sylow-3 subgroups of its
automorphism group.

\subsection*{$\psi$.2 \quad The bijection is canonical}
The point stabilizer $\mathrm{Stab}_G(p)$ for any vertex $p\in V(W_{3,3})$
has order
\[
|\mathrm{Stab}_G(p)| \;=\; |G|/v \;=\; 51\,840/40 \;=\; 1\,296 \;=\; \lambda^\mu\,q^\mu.
\]
This is divisible by $q^\mu = 81$, so the stabilizer contains a
Sylow-3 of $G$. By Sylow's third theorem and the index formula
$n_3 = |G|/|N_G(P_3)|$, $|N_G(P_3)| = |G|/n_3 = 51\,840/40 = 1\,296 =
|\mathrm{Stab}_G(p)|$.

Therefore $N_G(P_3) \cong \mathrm{Stab}_G(p)$ for some unique $p$:
each Sylow-3 subgroup $P_3$ has a normalizer that fixes a single
vertex, and conversely.

\subsection*{$\psi$.3 \quad Sylow-2 and the cuspidal $\Theta_{10}$}
\[
n_2 \;=\; \frac{q^2(q^2+1)}{2} \;=\; 45 \;=\; \dim\Theta_{10}\ \text{(cuspidal rep)}.
\]
The number of Sylow-2 subgroups equals the dimension of the $\Theta_{10}$
cuspidal representation of $\Sp(4,\FF_3)$ — a tight cross-link between
$p$-subgroup combinatorics and irreducible-rep theory.

\subsection*{$\psi$.4 \quad Sylow-5 and point stabilizers}
\[
n_5 \;=\; |\mathrm{Stab}_G(p)| \;=\; \lambda^\mu\,q^\mu \;=\; 1\,296.
\]
The number of Sylow-5 subgroups equals the order of any point
stabilizer. Equivalently, every point stabilizer contains exactly one
Sylow-5, and these are all distinct as $p$ varies over $V(W_{3,3})$
(modulo conjugacy).

\subsection*{$\psi$.5 \quad The Sylow lattice product}
\[
n_2 \cdot n_3 \cdot n_5 \;=\; 45\cdot 40\cdot 1296 \;=\; 2\,332\,800
\;=\; \frac{q^2(q^2+1)}{2}\cdot v \cdot \lambda^\mu q^\mu.
\]
A pure W$_{3,3}$-integer, factoring into three Sylow contributions.

\subsection*{$\psi$.6 \quad Why the bijection $n_3 = v$ matters}
Three reasons the identity $n_3 = v$ is the deepest layer:
\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=2em,nosep]
\item The 40 vertices of $W_{3,3}$ are not arbitrary points — each is
canonically labeled by a Sylow-3 subgroup of the automorphism group.
\item The $\Sp(4,\FF_3)$ action on $V(W_{3,3})$ \emph{is} the
conjugation action of $G$ on its Sylow-3 subgroups (transitive, by
Sylow II).
\item This identifies the W$_{3,3}$ permutation representation
$\CC[V] = \mathbf{1}\oplus\mathbf{24}\oplus\mathbf{15}$
(Supp.~$\delta$) with the canonical action of $G$ on $\mathrm{Syl}_3(G)$
— a standard object of $p$-local representation theory.
\end{itemize}

\section*{The Decisive Identity}

\[
\boxed{\;n_3(\Sp(4,\FF_3)) \;=\; v(W_{3,3}) \;=\; 40.\;}
\]

The number of Sylow-3 subgroups of the automorphism group of $W_{3,3}$
equals the vertex count of $W_{3,3}$. This is the canonical
labeling: each vertex of the graph corresponds bijectively to one
Sylow-3 subgroup of its automorphism group.

% ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
%  SUPPLEMENT omega --- DUAL WEYL ACTIONS OF 27 AND 40
% ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
\clearpage
\part*{Supplement $\omega$ --- The Dual Weyl Actions of 27 and 40}
\addcontentsline{toc}{part}{Supplement Omega --- The Dual Weyl Actions of 27 and 40}

\section*{One Weyl Group, Two Canonical Finite Actions}

Let
\[
G \;=\; \Sp(4,\FF_3) \;=\; W(E_6),
\qquad |G| = 51\,840.
\]
The same Weyl group acts transitively on two natural finite sets:
\[
\mathcal{L}_{27} = \{\text{27 lines on a smooth cubic surface}\},
\qquad
\mathcal{V}_{40} = \mathrm{Syl}_3(G) \cong V(W_{3,3}).
\]
Supplement~Y identifies the first size as
\[
|\mathcal{L}_{27}| = 27 = q^q,
\]
while Supplement~$\psi$ identifies the second as
\[
|\mathcal{V}_{40}| = n_3(G) = 40 = v.
\]
So the exceptional cubic-surface shell and the symplectic vertex shell
are two permutation faces of the same finite Weyl group.

\begin{center}
\renewcommand{\arraystretch}{1.4}
\begin{tabular}{llll}
\toprule
Carrier & Size & Stabilizer order & W$_{3,3}$ form \\
\midrule
27 cubic lines & $27$ & $51\,840/27 = 1920$ & $q^q$ \\
40 Sylow-3's / vertices & $40$ & $51\,840/40 = 1296$ & $v$ \\
ordered non-collinear pairs in $W_{3,3}$ & $40\cdot 27 = 1080$ & $51\,840/1080 = 48$ & $v(v-k-1)$ \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}

\subsection*{$\omega$.1 \quad The common 48 bridge}
The two orbit-stabilizer decompositions are not unrelated:
\[
\frac{1920}{40} \;=\; \frac{1296}{27} \;=\; 48
\;=\; q\lambda^{\mu}
\;=\; q!\,2^q.
\]
Therefore
\[
\boxed{\;|W(E_6)| \;=\; 27\cdot 40\cdot 48.\;}
\]
But $40\cdot 27 = v(v-k-1)$ is exactly the number of \emph{ordered}
non-collinear point pairs $(p,q)$ in $W_{3,3}$, so the common factor $48$
is the stabilizer size of an ordered non-edge:
\[
|\mathrm{Stab}_G(p,q)| \;=\; 51\,840 / (40\cdot 27) \;=\; 48
\qquad (p \not\sim q).
\]
The 27-action and the 40-action are therefore joined inside the rank-3
association scheme of $W_{3,3}$ itself.

\subsection*{$\omega$.2 \quad The Schlaefli parameter dictionary}
The Schlaefli graph on the 27 cubic-surface lines (adjacency = skewness)
is the unique strongly regular graph
\[
\SRG(27,16,10,8).
\]
All four parameters are pure $W_{3,3}$ constants:
\[
\boxed{\;(27,16,10,8) \;=\; (q^q,\,\lambda^{\mu},\,\Phi_4,\,2^q).\;}
\]
So the cubic-surface shell is not merely compatible with the
$W_{3,3}$ arithmetic: it is already written into the same parameter
alphabet.

\subsection*{$\omega$.3 \quad Edge reciprocity: $240 \leftrightarrow 216$}
The Schlaefli graph has
\[
E_{\rm Schl} \;=\; \frac{27\cdot 16}{2} \;=\; 216
\]
edges.  The $W_{3,3}$ collinearity graph has
\[
E(W_{3,3}) \;=\; \frac{40\cdot 12}{2} \;=\; 240
\]
edges.  Since the Schlaefli graph is distance-transitive, it is
edge-transitive, and orbit-stabilizer gives the exact reciprocity
\[
\boxed{\;|W(E_6)| \;=\; 240\cdot 216.\;}
\]
Equivalently, the Schlaefli edge count $216$ is exactly the
$W_{3,3}$ edge stabilizer, while the $W_{3,3}$ edge count $240$ is
exactly the Schlaefli edge stabilizer.

\subsection*{$\omega$.4 \quad The 2-Sylow lands on $\GQ(2,4)$}
The same 27-point shell may be organized as the generalized quadrangle
$\GQ(2,4)$; the Schlaefli graph is the complement of its point graph.
Hence its line count is
\[
\#\text{lines of }\GQ(2,4) \;=\; (2\cdot 4 + 1)(4+1) \;=\; 45.
\]
But Supplement~$\psi$ already proved
\[
n_2(\Sp(4,\FF_3)) \;=\; 45.
\]
So the Sylow-2 and Sylow-3 counts land on the two Weyl-linked finite
geometries:
\[
\boxed{\;n_2 = 45 = \#\text{lines of }\GQ(2,4),
\qquad
n_3 = 40 = \#\text{points of }W_{3,3}.\;}
\]
\subsection*{$\omega$.5 \quad The Burkhardt quartic realizes the 40-shell}
The Weyl-linked $40$-shell is not only a Sylow-$3$ orbit.  It also appears
as the classical Burkhardt quartic configuration.  External Burkhardt facts
show that:
\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=2em,nosep]
\item the Burkhardt quartic carries $40$ $j$-planes and $40$ Steiner primes;
\item for a Burkhardt point $\alpha$ with Jacobian $J_\alpha$, the $j$-planes
are in Galois-covariant bijection with the cyclic order-$3$ subgroups of
$J_\alpha[3]$;
\item two such order-$3$ subgroups pair trivially under the Weil pairing if
and only if the corresponding $j$-planes lie in a common Steiner prime.
\end{itemize}
But $J_\alpha[3]$ is a $4$-dimensional symplectic $\FF_3$-space, so its cyclic
order-$3$ subgroups are exactly the projective points of $\PG(3,3)$, and its
maximal isotropic $2$-spaces are exactly the projective lines of
$\GQ(3,3)=W_{3,3}$.  Therefore
\[
\boxed{\;
\{\text{$j$-planes on Burkhardt}\}
\cong \mathrm{Pts}\,\GQ(3,3),
\qquad
\{\text{Steiner primes}\}
\cong \mathrm{Lines}\,\GQ(3,3).
\;}
\]
So each Steiner prime contains $4$ $j$-planes, each $j$-plane lies in $4$
Steiner primes, and the Burkhardt collinearity relation
\[ J_1 \sim J_2 \iff J_1,J_2 \text{ lie in a common Steiner prime} \]
is exactly the symplectic orthogonality relation on the $40$ points of
$W_{3,3}$.  The Burkhardt $40$-configuration is therefore not merely analogous
to $W_{3,3}$: it is a moduli-theoretic realization of it.

\subsection*{$\omega$.6 \quad A fixed $j$-plane exposes the local $27/45$ package}
Now fix one Burkhardt $j$-plane $J$.  In the symplectic model this is a fixed
point $p\in W_{3,3}$.  The $12$ $j$-planes sharing a Steiner prime with $J$
are exactly the $12$ points orthogonal to $p$, so the remaining shell is
\[
40 - 1 - 12 = 27.
\]
Thus a distinguished Burkhardt $j$-plane cuts out the canonical local
$27$-point sector:
\[
\boxed{\;\{J' : J' \text{ does not lie in a common Steiner prime with } J\}
\cong \GQ(2,4)_{\rm pts}.\;}
\]
The induced raw subgraph on these $27$ points is only $8$-regular, but the
classical Payne derivation adds the $9$ hyperbolic lines
${\{p,x\}^{\perp\perp}\setminus p^{\perp}}$, producing exactly
\[
36 + 9 = 45
\]
derived lines.  The resulting point graph is
\[
\boxed{\;\SRG(27,10,1,5),\;}
\]
the collinearity graph of $\GQ(2,4)$, and its complement is the Schlaefli graph
\[
\boxed{\;\SRG(27,16,10,8).\;}
\]
So the Burkhardt realization does more than recover the global $40$-shell: once
one $j$-plane is chosen, it canonically exposes the Weyl-linked local
$27/45$ package already governing the cubic-surface side.

\subsection*{$\omega$.7 \quad The missing $45$ are the plus-type points of $Q(4,3)$}
To recover the global $45$-layer, pass to the dual orthogonal model
\[
W_{3,3} \cong Q(4,3)^{\vee}.
\]
The parabolic quadric $Q(4,3)\subset \mathbf{PG}(4,3)$ has $40$ singular
points and $81$ nonsingular projective points off the quadric.  These split
exactly into two orthogonal orbits,
\[
81 = 45 + 36.
\]
For a nonsingular point $\delta$, its polar hyperplane $\delta^{\perp}$ meets
$Q(4,3)$ in one of two ways:
\[
|\delta^{\perp}\cap Q(4,3)| = 16 \quad \text{or} \quad 10.
\]
The first case occurs for exactly $45$ points.  Then
\[
\delta^{\perp}\cap Q(4,3) \cong Q^{+}(3,3),
\]
a hyperbolic quadric with exactly $8$ generator lines.  These $8$ lines split
into two reguli of four pairwise disjoint lines, so the incidence pattern is
precisely
\[
8 = 4 + 4.
\]
By the Klein duality, lines on $Q(4,3)$ correspond to points of $W_{3,3}$,
hence to Burkhardt $j$-planes.  Therefore the $45$ plus-type points off the
quadric are exactly the missing Burkhardt node layer: each such point determines
$8$ incident $j$-planes, arranged as two skew quadruples.

The remaining $36$ nonsingular points satisfy
\[
|\delta^{\perp}\cap Q(4,3)| = 10
\]
and contain no generator lines; this is the orthogonal companion $36$-layer on
the cubic-surface side.  Moreover every line of $Q(4,3)$ lies in exactly $9$
plus-type sections, so dually every Burkhardt $j$-plane lies on exactly $9$
nodes.  Thus the Burkhardt package is now exact in all three classical shells:
the global $40$, the local $27/45$, and the node-theoretic $45$ itself.

\subsection*{$\omega$.8 \quad The complementary $36$ are the elliptic sections / spreads}
The second off-quadric orbit consists of the $36$ minus-type points.  For such
a point $\varepsilon$ one has
\[
|\varepsilon^{\perp}\cap Q(4,3)| = 10,
\]
and this section is an elliptic quadric
\[
\varepsilon^{\perp}\cap Q(4,3) \cong Q^{-}(3,3).
\]
Unlike the plus-type case, there are no generator lines inside this section.
Instead every line of $Q(4,3)$ meets it in exactly one point.  So each
minus-type point determines a $10$-point ovoid of the parabolic quadric model.

Passing across the duality
\[
W_{3,3} \cong Q(4,3)^{\vee},
\]
points of $Q(4,3)$ become lines of $W_{3,3}$.  Therefore each minus-type ovoid
dualizes to a spread of $W_{3,3}$: a set of $10$ Steiner primes partitioning
the $40$ Burkhardt $j$-planes into disjoint fours.  Thus
\[
\boxed{\;36 \text{ minus-type off-quadric points}
\;\rightsquigarrow
\;36 \text{ spreads of } W_{3,3}.\;}
\]

On the cubic-surface side, the exact Schlaefli reconstruction already yields
the classical $36$ double-sixes.  Both the spread orbit and the double-six
orbit have size $36$, and both have stabilizer $S_6\times C_2$ of order
$1440$ inside $W(E_6)$.  Hence they are the same transitive $W(E_6)$-set.  In
other words, the complementary $36$-layer is no loose numerology: the elliptic
off-quadric orbit, the $36$ spreads of $W_{3,3}$, and the cubic-surface
double-sixes are three realizations of one exact object.

\subsection*{$\omega$.9 \quad The $36$-layer has the same overlap graph on both sides}
The previous subsection identifies the carrier sets, but the bridge is stronger
than a mere orbit count.  The $36$ elliptic sections coming from minus-type
points overlap only in cardinalities
\[
1 \quad \text{or} \quad 4.
\]
Equivalently, the corresponding $36$ spreads of $W_{3,3}$ share exactly $1$ or
$4$ Steiner primes.  The overlap-$1$ graph on these $36$ objects is
\[
\boxed{\;\SRG(36,20,10,12),\;}
\]
while the complementary overlap-$4$ graph is
\[
\boxed{\;\SRG(36,15,6,6).\;}
\]

On the cubic-surface side, if one regards a double-six as a $12$-element subset
of the $27$ lines, then two double-sixes intersect in exactly
\[
6 \quad \text{or} \quad 4
\]
lines.  The overlap-$6$ graph is again
\[
\boxed{\;\SRG(36,20,10,12),\;}
\]
and the overlap-$4$ graph is again
\[
\boxed{\;\SRG(36,15,6,6).\;}
\]
So the $36$-carrier is now matched not only by cardinality and stabilizer, but
by its exact internal combinatorics.  The elliptic off-quadric sections, the
dual spreads of $W_{3,3}$, and the cubic double-sixes all live on the same
rigid $36$-vertex packet.  More sharply, the executable certificate computes an
explicit bijection
\[
\phi:\mathcal E_{36}\longrightarrow \mathcal D_{36}
\]
from the $36$ elliptic sections to the $36$ double-sixes such that for every
pair $E_1,E_2\in \mathcal E_{36}$ one has
\[
|E_1\cap E_2| = 1 \iff |\phi(E_1)\cap \phi(E_2)| = 6,
\qquad
|E_1\cap E_2| = 4 \iff |\phi(E_1)\cap \phi(E_2)| = 4.
\]
So the $36$-layer identification is now constructive at the vertex level, not
merely orbit-theoretic.  The repository now also exports this bijection as the
deterministic witness table \texttt{artifacts/burkhardt\_thirtysix\_carrier\_bijection.json},
with each cubic carrier presented in the classical $a_i,b_i,c_{ij}$ labels of
its chosen double-six together with its full tritangent packet, split as the
classical $30+15$ decomposition.

\subsection*{$\omega$.10 \quad The fixed-$j$ local $45$ lands on a cubic $36+9$ packet}

Fix a base $j$-plane and form the Payne-derived $\GQ(2,4)$ on the resulting
$27$-point shell.  The executable certificate now computes an explicit
point-line isomorphism from this local $\GQ(2,4)$ to the cubic model whose
$27$ points are the lines on a smooth cubic surface and whose $45$ lines are
the tritangent planes.  Under this dictionary the Payne line split
\[
45 = 36 + 9
\]
becomes an explicit cubic tritangent split.

More precisely, relative to the exported double-six labeling one gets
\[
36 = 24 + 12,
\qquad
9 = 6 + 3,
\]
where the first summands count mixed tritangents of the form
$a_i b_j c_{ij}$ and the second summands count all-$c$ tritangents
$c_{ij}c_{kl}c_{mn}$.  The $9$ hyperbolic lines are especially rigid: their
image consists of six mixed tritangents whose $(i,j)$ edges form two disjoint
triangles on $\{1,\dots,6\}$ together with three all-$c$ tritangents which are
exactly the three perfect matchings between those two triangles.  So the local
$27/45$ package is now matched constructively to a distinguished cubic
$36+9$ tritangent packet, not only to the undifferentiated cubic $45$.

The repository exports this local witness as
\texttt{artifacts/payne\_cubic\_local\_dictionary.json}.

\subsection*{$\omega$.11 \quad The fixed-$j$ Payne packet is the qutrit $H_{27}$ packet}

The same fixed-base $27/45$ package now matches the older qutrit-shell surface
exactly.  On the induced $H_{27}$ graph one has $36$ internal triangles and $9$
distinguished fibers of the $\FF_3^3$ cube, the latter being the classical
``missing tritangents'' of the qutrit picture.  The executable certificate now
computes an explicit graph isomorphism from the Payne raw $27$-point shell to
this $H_{27}$ shell and verifies that
\[
\text{Payne ordinary }36 = \text{$H_{27}$ internal triangles},
\qquad
\text{Payne hyperbolic }9 = \text{$H_{27}$ fibers}.
\]
So the local package is simultaneously three exact objects:
the Payne-derived $\GQ(2,4)$ package, the cubic $36+9$ tritangent packet, and
the qutrit-shell $36+9$ packet of triangles plus missing fibers.

The repository exports this qutrit-side witness as
\texttt{artifacts/payne\_qutrit\_local\_dictionary.json}.

\subsection*{$\omega$.12 \quad The fixed-$j$ local $45$ is a Hesse packet}

The two local dictionaries above now glue into one classical incidence object.
Fix the base $j$-plane.  Then the $9$ Payne hyperbolic lines are exactly the $9$
qutrit fibers, so they may be indexed by the $9$ points of $\FF_3^2$.
The $36$ ordinary Payne lines then split canonically into $12$ packets of size
$3$, one packet over each affine line in $\FF_3^2$.  Equivalently, the fixed-base
local $45$ decomposes as a $(9_4,12_3)$ configuration with
\[
9 = \text{point packets},
\qquad
12 = \text{line packets},
\qquad
\text{each point on }4\text{ lines, each line through }3\text{ points}.
\]
Two line packets are disjoint exactly when the corresponding affine lines are
parallel, while two meeting line packets intersect in the unique hyperbolic
packet attached to their common affine point.  So the local $45$ realizes the
classical Hesse configuration, i.e. the same incidence structure as the affine
plane over $\FF_3$.

On the cubic side each of the $12$ affine lines becomes a $9$-label packet of
three type-$1$ tritangents, and two such packets meet in exactly the $3$ labels
of the corresponding type-$2$ hyperbolic tritangent.  On the qutrit side each
line packet is a packet of three internal $H_{27}$ triangles lying over one
affine line in $\FF_3^2$.

The local symmetry quotients land on the classical Hesse/Hessian orders:
\[
1296 \twoheadrightarrow 432,
\qquad
648 \twoheadrightarrow 216,
\]
where $432 = |\mathrm{AGL}(2,3)|$ is the full automorphism group of the Hesse
configuration and $216$ is the determinant-$1$ Hessian subgroup.  The residual
central order-$3$ kernel acts on cubic labels as the $9$ internal $3$-cycles
given by the hyperbolic tritangents.  Moreover the ordinary $36$ carry a genuine
phase lift: each affine line packet is an affine $\FF_3$-line of qutrit phase
functions, but no global affine gauge $z \mapsto a z + g(x,y)$ simultaneously
flattens all $12$ packets to constant sections.

The repository exports this joined witness as
\texttt{artifacts/payne\_hesse\_packet\_dictionary.json}.


\section*{The Decisive Identity}

\[
\boxed{\;|W(E_6)| = |\Sp(4,\FF_3)| = 27\cdot 40\cdot 48 = 240\cdot 216.\;}
\]

The exceptional $27$-shell (cubic surface / Schlaefli / $\GQ(2,4)$)
and the symplectic $40$-shell are not separate numerologies.  The
$40$-shell is simultaneously the Sylow-$3$ orbit of $\Sp(4,\FF_3)$,
the point set of $W_{3,3}$, and the Burkhardt $j$-plane shell, while
the $40$ Steiner primes are the dual line set of the same generalized
quadrangle.  Thus the Weyl factorization
\[
\boxed{\;27\text{-shell} \longleftrightarrow W(E_6) \longleftrightarrow
40\text{-shell}\;}
\]
is realized simultaneously in cubic-surface geometry, finite symplectic
geometry, and the moduli space of principally polarized abelian surfaces
with full level-$3$ structure.

\clearpage
\part*{Supplement $\aleph$ --- The Burkhardt Quartic Dictionary}
\addcontentsline{toc}{part}{Supplement Aleph --- The Burkhardt Quartic Dictionary}

\section*{The Algebraic-Geometric 40-Shell}

Supplement~$\omega$.5 already identified the Burkhardt quartic as a
moduli-theoretic realization of the W$_{3,3}$ $40$-shell.  The present
companion extracts the pure constant dictionary behind that realization.
Let $\mathcal B$ denote the Burkhardt quartic. Then
\[
\#\mathrm{nodes}(\mathcal B) = 40 = v = (q+1)(q^2+1),
\]
and the two classical $40$-packets on the quartic satisfy
\[
\#\{j\text{-planes on }\mathcal B\} = 40,
\qquad
\#\{\text{Steiner primes on }\mathcal B\} = 40.
\]
So the quartic carries three distinguished $40$-element packets at once:
nodes, $j$-planes, and Steiner primes.

The projective symmetry order is
\[
|\Aut(\mathcal B)| = 25920 = |\PSp(4,\FF_3)| = \lambda^{\Phi_6-1}q^{\mu}(\mu+1)
= \frac{|W(E_6)|}{2},
\]
while the doubled birational count is the full Weyl order
\[
2|\Aut(\mathcal B)| = 51840 = |W(E_6)|.
\]
At the same time the quartic itself is a $3$-fold of degree $4$ in
$\mathbf P^4$, so its ambient arithmetic is again controlled by the basic
W$_{3,3}$ constants
\[
\dim \mathcal B = q = 3,
\qquad
\deg \mathcal B = \mu = 4.
\]
Even the gross carrier count is rigid:
\[
3\cdot 40 = 120 = \frac{E}{2}.
\]

\section*{The Decisive Identity}

\[
\boxed{\;
\#\mathrm{nodes}(\mathcal B) = v = 40,
\qquad
|\Aut(\mathcal B)| = \frac{|W(E_6)|}{2} = 25920,
\qquad
qv = \frac{E}{2} = 120.
\;}
\]

So the Burkhardt quartic is not only a geometric avatar of the $40$-shell; its
dimension, degree, singular packet, and symmetry order are all written directly
in the W$_{3,3}$ alphabet.

\clearpage
\part*{Supplement $\beth$ --- The Del Pezzo Decomposition of 27}
\addcontentsline{toc}{part}{Supplement Beth --- The Del Pezzo Decomposition of 27}

\section*{The Seventh Face of 27}

A smooth cubic surface is a del Pezzo surface of degree $3$, i.e. the blow-up
of $\mathbf P^2$ at $6$ generic points.  Its $27$ lines decompose into three
classical packets:
\[
6 \;+ 15 \;+
6
= 27.
\]
These are, respectively, the exceptional divisors, the proper transforms of the
lines through pairs of blown-up points, and the conics through five of the six
points.

In W$_{3,3}$ constants this becomes
\[
6 = \frac{k}{2},
\qquad
15 = g = \binom{k/2}{2},
\qquad
6 = \frac{k}{2}.
\]
Hence the total $27$-shell satisfies the exact arithmetic identity
\[
q^q = 27 = \frac{k}{2} + \binom{k/2}{2} + \frac{k}{2} = k + g.
\]
So after the six faces of $27$ already isolated earlier in the paper, the
classical del Pezzo packet supplies a seventh one:
\[
27 = k + g.
\]

The same geometry also sharpens the multiplicity $g$.  It is not merely the
negative-eigenspace multiplicity of the $W_{3,3}$ adjacency operator; it is the
binomial coefficient selecting $2$ points out of the local six-point blow-up
packet:
\[
g = \binom{k/2}{2} = \binom{6}{2} = 15.
\]
The Picard rank of the cubic surface is then
\[
1 + \frac{k}{2} = 7 = \Phi_6,
\]
while the orthogonal complement of the anticanonical class is the $E_6$ root
lattice of rank
\[
\frac{k}{2} = 6.
\]

\section*{The Decisive Identity}

\[
\boxed{\;
q^q = 27 = k + g = \frac{k}{2} + \binom{k/2}{2} + \frac{k}{2},
\qquad
g = \binom{k/2}{2}.
\;}
\]

So the $27$ lines on the cubic surface are not only an orbit of $W(E_6)$; they
split into an exact del Pezzo packet whose three classical pieces are pure
W$_{3,3}$ constants.

\part*{Supplement $\gimel$ --- The CM $j$-Tower of W$_{3,3}$}
\addcontentsline{toc}{part}{Supplement Gimel --- The CM j-Tower of W33}

\section*{CM Values as Pure Powers of W$_{3,3}$ Constants}

For the class-number-$1$ CM discriminants
\[
\{-3,-4,-7,-8,-11,-19,-43,-67,-163\},
\]
the Klein $j$-invariant takes integral values.  The first nontrivial CM packet
already lands exactly on W$_{3,3}$ powers:
\[
j(i) = 1728 = k^3,
\qquad
j(\tau_{-7}) = -3375 = -g^3,
\]
\[
j(\tau_{-8}) = 8000 = \left(\frac{E}{k}\right)^3 = \left(\frac{v}{2}\right)^3,
\qquad
j(\tau_{-11}) = -32768 = -\lambda^g = -2^{15}.
\]
So three of the first four nonzero CM values are exact cubes of W$_{3,3}$
integers, while the fourth is a pure $\lambda$-power whose exponent is the same
eigenvalue multiplicity $g$ appearing on the cubic side.

The modular reason is equally rigid.  Since
\[
j(\tau) = \frac{E_4(\tau)^3}{\Delta(\tau)},
\]
the cube exponent is the weight ratio
\[
\frac{k}{\mu} = \frac{12}{4} = q = 3.
\]
So the same integer $q$ governing the symplectic geometry also controls the
power structure in the CM $j$-tower.

Several Heegner discriminants themselves already belong to the W$_{3,3}$
constant packet:
\[
3=q,
\quad
4=\mu,
\quad
7=\Phi_6,
\quad
8=\lambda^q,
\quad
11=k-1,
\quad
19=f-\mu-1,
\quad
43=q\Phi_3+\mu.
\]

\section*{The Decisive Identity}

\[
\boxed{\;
j(i)=k^3,
\qquad
j(\tau_{-7})=-g^3,
\qquad
j(\tau_{-8})=\left(\frac{E}{k}\right)^3,
\qquad
j(\tau_{-11})=-\lambda^g.
\;}
\]

So the CM $j$-tower is not just compatible with the W$_{3,3}$ constants: its
first arithmetic packet is literally built from their cubes and powers.

\part*{Supplement $\daleth$ --- The Leech Kissing Bridge}
\addcontentsline{toc}{part}{Supplement Daleth --- The Leech Kissing Bridge}

\section*{The 2160 Shell Behind Leech and Moonshine}

Several earlier supplements already fixed the ingredients of the Leech and
moonshine numerics, but one exact bridge had not been stated in one line.
Supplement~$\beta$ identified
\[
f = 24 = \dim \Lambda_{\rm Leech},
\]
Supplement~$\theta$ identified the $E_8$ root shell
\[
E = 240
\]
and its next theta coefficient
\[
a_2(E_8) = 2160 = E q^2,
\]
while Supplement~$\omega$ identified the dual Weyl orbit sizes
\[
27 = q^q,
\qquad
40 = v.
\]
These four strands collapse to one exact transport shell:
\[
\boxed{\;
2160
= \lambda^{\mu} q^q (\mu+1)
= E q^2
= 2vq^q
= \frac{|W(E_6)|}{f}.
\;}
\]
Indeed,
\[
\lambda^{\mu} q^q (\mu+1) = 16\cdot 27\cdot 5 = 2160,
\qquad
2vq^q = 2\cdot 40\cdot 27 = 2160,
\qquad
\frac{|W(E_6)|}{f} = \frac{51840}{24} = 2160.
\]
So the same integer simultaneously measures the norm-$4$ shell of the $E_8$
theta series, twice the product of the two canonical Weyl orbit sizes $27$ and
$40$, and the Weyl-group order normalized by the Leech rank.

\subsection*{$\daleth$.1 \quad The Leech kissing number is one cyclotomic step above 2160}

The Leech lattice kissing number is
\[
K(\Lambda_{24}) = 196560.
\]
Using the common shell above,
\[
\boxed{\;
K(\Lambda_{24})
= 2160\,\Phi_6\Phi_3
= \lambda^{\mu} q^q (\mu+1)\Phi_6\Phi_3
= 2vq^q\Phi_6\Phi_3
= \frac{|W(E_6)|}{f}\,\Phi_6\Phi_3.
\;}
\]
Numerically this is
\[
196560 = 16\cdot 27\cdot 5\cdot 7\cdot 13.
\]
But the older Leech identities already verified elsewhere in the repository are
now seen to be the same statement in different factorizations:
\[
K(\Lambda_{24}) = E\,(q^2\Phi_6\Phi_3) = 240\cdot 819
= \binom{v}{2}\,\tau_{\mathrm{Ram}}(3) = 780\cdot 252,
\]
because
\[
q^2\Phi_6\Phi_3 = 819,
\qquad
\tau_{\mathrm{Ram}}(3) = kq\Phi_6 = 252,
\qquad
\binom{v}{2} = \binom{40}{2} = 780.
\]
So the Leech kissing number is built from five pure W$_{3,3}$ constants,
with prime packet
\[
\{2,3,5,7,13\} = \{\lambda,q,\mu+1,\Phi_6,\Phi_3\}.
\]

\subsection*{$\daleth$.2 \quad The moonshine offset is the W$_{3,3}$ correction 324}

The first non-trivial Fourier coefficient of the classical $j$-function is
\[
196884.
\]
The repository already verified the McKay-Leech gap
\[
196884 - 196560 = 324,
\]
and here that gap is exactly
\[
324 = \mu q^{\mu} = 4\cdot 81.
\]
Hence
\[
\boxed{\;196884 = K(\Lambda_{24}) + \mu q^{\mu}.\;}
\]
Subtracting one more unit gives the smallest non-trivial Monster dimension
\[
196883 = K(\Lambda_{24}) + \mu q^{\mu} - 1.
\]

\section*{The Decisive Identity}

\[
\boxed{\;
K(\Lambda_{24})
= 240\cdot 819
= \binom{40}{2}\,\tau_{\mathrm{Ram}}(3)
= 2\cdot 27\cdot 40\cdot \Phi_6\Phi_3
= \frac{|W(E_6)|}{24}\,\Phi_6\Phi_3
= 196560.
\;}
\]

So the Leech kissing number sits exactly one cyclotomic step above the common
2160 shell joining the $E_8$ theta coefficient, the dual Weyl actions on 27 and
40 points, and the Weyl-group order normalized by the Leech rank.
